A resource runs its instances where it ran an instance with no task, after every due task, once a cycle, in the order of the `program` lines, and reports each run under its name, `{:ran, "main", "boxes", now}`, where without the line it is `:none`; `Logex.Runtime.next_due_in/1` counts it not, and `overlaps/1` lists it at 0, as an event task alone (M4-2). The runner runs it in rounds back to back (decision 108), its watchdog timing each round (decision 111). A continuous line that runs no instance is the warning a task that runs none is. Adding or removing the line while the plant runs is adding or removing a task, refused (decisions 19 and 111), and so is a task made continuous or timed; an edit that adds `with main` or takes it away moves nothing (decision 129). M4-2 reserves the word, in any case, in a configuration file only (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8), as a word of `Logex.Configuration.Text.keywords/0` that belongs on a `task` line: no line starts with it, and no task, global, program instance, program type or `var_external` is named by it. A `.ld` program may still name a tag of another section `continuous`. |
| IEC 61131-3 | none: a **PROGRAM with no TASK association** | Ed 2 §2.7.2 rule 4 (p.115): *"A program with no task association shall have the lowest system priority. Any such program shall be scheduled for execution upon “starting” of its resource, as defined in 1.4.1, and shall be re-scheduled for execution as soon as its execution terminates."*; Table 49 feature 6c, *"PROGRAM declaration with no TASK association"* (p.112). Ed 3 §6.8.2 d) (p.181) the same words, but for *"as defined in 1.4.1"*. No task is declared for it, and no keyword names it. Ed 4: `unverified`. |
| Conventional | a **Continuous** task; `Type := CONTINUOUS` in its text export | *"The continuous task runs in the background. Any CPU time not allocated to other operations (such as motion, communication, and periodic or event tasks) is used to run the programs within the continuous task."*; *"The continuous task runs all the time. When the continuous task completes a full scan, it restarts immediately."*; *"A project does not require a continuous task. If used, there can be only one continuous task."* (tasks manual, Sept 2025, p.8); it takes no priority and *"always runs at the lowest priority"* (p.13). The text export writes `TASK MainTask (Type := CONTINUOUS, …, Watchdog := 500, …)` (import/export reference, Sept 2025, p.69), and *"Specify whether the type of task is Continuous, Periodic, or Event. There can be only one continuous task."* (p.313). |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | the **cyclic program**, a program cycle OB (OB 1) | *"In the simplest case, you execute the entire user program in the cyclic program of the CPU."*, and *"All cycle OBs always have the lowest priority of 1."* (SIMATIC S7-1500 *Cycle and response times*, A5E03461504-02, 02/2014, pp.8–9). |
| CODESYS | a task of type **Freewheeling** | *"Processing of the task restarts automatically in a continuous loop at program start and after the end of a run after a certain waiting time"*, *"The duration is a percentage of the last cycle duration."*, and *"You do not define a cycle time."* (*Object: Task*, Type). |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | a **scan execution type** program | *"Scan execution type program is executed only once per every scan starting from the scan following the scan in which the initial execution type program was executed."* (SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR §1.5, p.60). |

**Chosen:** `continuous`
**Why:** IEC declares no task for the programs that run back to back, and so names none, so rule 1 gives nothing and rule 2 takes the clearest vendor word: the conventional family's Continuous task, which its text export spells `CONTINUOUS`, rather than CODESYS's Freewheeling, which waits between runs, or Siemens' and Mitsubishi's phrases. Rule 3 keeps logex from coining `background` or `idle`. It is a flag, not an input with a value, since the conventional family's task type is a choice among three and logex's other two kinds are what `interval` and `single` already say; it goes straight after the name, so that a line says what kind of task it declares before anything else, and it takes none of IEC's inputs, as the conventional continuous task takes no period, trigger or priority. A configuration declares it only to name it and give it a watchdog (decision 111): without the line every instance with no `with` still runs as IEC's rule 4 has it. One at most, as the conventional family's.
**Checked:** 2026-10-18. IEC 61131-3:2003 §2.7.2 (pp.114–115) and Table 49, and IEC 61131-3:2013 §6.8.2 (p.181), read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists; the conventional family's tasks manual and import/export reference (both Sept 2025), in the copies Milestone 2's survey saved; Siemens SIMATIC S7-1500 *Cycle and response times*, A5E03461504-02; CODESYS online help, *Object: Task*, as saved on 2026-10-02; Mitsubishi SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR. IEC 61131-3:2025 was not read.

### `sint` — the 8-bit signed integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var boxes_in_case sint`; `var_global trim sint -5` | -128 to 127, two's complement in 8 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. Declared wherever a dint is: a tag of any section, `var_external` among them, a block's member, a global, located or not, a host's input, a device's value and a force, each checked against its range. `move` widens it into `int dint lint` (Ed 3 Figure 11); an arithmetic or comparison slot reads a dint alone, so `sint_to_dint` comes first until those instructions take every width (`PLAN.md` M5-4); anything else takes a conversion, `sint_to_usint` among them, which keeps the low bits of a value that does not fit and passes no power on that scan. A literal moved into one, given to a `cal` formal or connected to a var_input of its type must fit it. A `var_external` binds only to a global of exactly its type. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **SINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 2, "Short integer", N = 8, footnote c: the range is −2^(N−1) to 2^(N−1) − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 2 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into `int dint lint` and, among the reals, `real` and `lreal`; every other conversion is explicit, a `SINT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_SINT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **SINT** | *"SINT 1-byte integer -128 to 127"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), on every controller family the reference lists. Narrowing into it keeps the low bits: *"DINT 65,665 … SINT 16#81 (-127)"* (p.872), the example this item's tests pin. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **SINT** in TIA's reference, **SInt** in the S7-1200 manual; none in classic STEP 7 | *"An operand of data type SINT (Short INT) has a length of 8 bits and consists of two components: a sign and a numerical value in the two's complement"*, -128 to +127 (TIA V21, *SINT (8-bit integers)*); S7-1200 A5E02486680-05 Table 4-17 p.88. |
| CODESYS | **SINT** | -128 to 127 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | `unverified`: SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR was not re-read for this stanza; the `dint` stanza found only "Word [Signed]" (INT) and "Double Word [Signed]" (DINT) there. |

**Chosen:** `sint`
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the type SINT, and every dialect surveyed that has it spells it so (decision 144); logex lowercases it, as it does `bool` and `dint`. It is the signed integer of 8 bits, its range IEC's footnote c, two's complement for a signed type as Siemens says in words and the conventional reference shows (p.871, 16#FFFF read as −1). Signed and unsigned stay separate types, as in every system read, and IEC's Figure 11 decides what converts without a word (decision 147): never signed into unsigned, nor unsigned into a signed type of its own width, and never narrowing. Each of the seven words is reserved in any case where a type word is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8).
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 10 (pp.31–32), Figure 11 (p.67) and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 Tables 10 and 13, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference (Sept 2025) pp.870–875 and design considerations manual (Sept 2025) p.24, and the Logix Designer 33.01 release notes, in the copies that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types* and Siemens A5E02486680-05; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer data types*. Mitsubishi was not re-read.
*(Amended 2026-10-22, M5-4, `docs/organisation.md` decision 147: the arithmetic and the comparisons take it as they take a dint, so the conversion to a dint before one is no longer needed: `add` and its kin compute in their destination's type, a source of this type taken where Figure 11 widens it into that type, and a comparison reads it beside an integer of any width by value.)*

### `int` — the 16-bit signed integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var small_count int`; `var_global fan_limit int 900` | -32768 to 32767, two's complement in 16 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. Declared wherever a dint is: a tag of any section, `var_external` among them, a block's member, a global, located or not, a host's input, a device's value and a force, each checked against its range. `move` widens it into `dint lint` (Ed 3 Figure 11); an arithmetic or comparison slot reads a dint alone, so `int_to_dint` comes first until those instructions take every width (`PLAN.md` M5-4); anything else takes a conversion, `int_to_sint` or `int_to_uint` among them, which keeps the low bits of a value that does not fit and passes no power on that scan. A literal moved into one, given to a `cal` formal or connected to a var_input of its type must fit it. A `var_external` binds only to a global of exactly its type. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **INT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 3, "Integer", N = 16, footnote c: the range is −2^(N−1) to 2^(N−1) − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 3 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into `dint lint` and, among the reals, `real` and `lreal`; every other conversion is explicit, a `INT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_INT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **INT** | *"INT 2-byte integer -32,768 to 32,767"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), on every controller family the reference lists. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **INT** in TIA's reference, **Int** in the S7-1200 manual; classic **INT** | -32,768 to +32,767 (S7-1200 A5E02486680-05 Table 4-17 p.88); TIA V21's own INT page was not re-read. Default 0 (*Layout of the block interface*, the predefined-value table's Int row). |
| CODESYS | **INT** | -32768 to 32767 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | Carried from the `dint` stanza: GX Works3 shows INT as "Word [Signed]" (SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR §23.4 p.422). Its other integer types' names there: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |

**Chosen:** `int`
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the type INT, and every dialect surveyed that has it spells it so (decision 144); logex lowercases it, as it does `bool` and `dint`. It is the signed integer of 16 bits, its range IEC's footnote c, two's complement for a signed type as Siemens says in words and the conventional reference shows (p.871, 16#FFFF read as −1). Signed and unsigned stay separate types, as in every system read, and IEC's Figure 11 decides what converts without a word (decision 147): never signed into unsigned, nor unsigned into a signed type of its own width, and never narrowing. It is reserved in any case, in every `.ld` and `.logex` file, so a tag, label, global or instance named `int`, `Int` or `INT` stops compiling: the likeliest of the seven to break a program, which the commit that reserves it says. Each of the seven words is reserved in any case where a type word is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8).
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 10 (pp.31–32), Figure 11 (p.67) and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 Tables 10 and 13, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference (Sept 2025) pp.870–875 and design considerations manual (Sept 2025) p.24, and the Logix Designer 33.01 release notes, in the copies that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types* and Siemens A5E02486680-05; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer data types*. Mitsubishi was not re-read.
*(Amended 2026-10-22, M5-4, `docs/organisation.md` decision 147: the arithmetic and the comparisons take it as they take a dint, so the conversion to a dint before one is no longer needed: `add` and its kin compute in their destination's type, a source of this type taken where Figure 11 widens it into that type, and a comparison reads it beside an integer of any width by value.)*

### `lint` — the 64-bit signed integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var day_total lint`; `var_global meter lint` | -9223372036854775808 to 9223372036854775807, two's complement in 64 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. Declared wherever a dint is: a tag of any section, `var_external` among them, a block's member, a global, located or not, a host's input, a device's value and a force, each checked against its range. It widens into no other integer type (Ed 3 Figure 11); an arithmetic or comparison slot reads a dint alone, so `lint_to_dint` comes first until those instructions take every width (`PLAN.md` M5-4); anything else takes a conversion, `lint_to_dint` among them, which keeps the low bits of a value that does not fit and passes no power on that scan. A literal moved into one, given to a `cal` formal or connected to a var_input of its type must fit it. A `var_external` binds only to a global of exactly its type. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **LINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 5, "Long integer", N = 64, footnote c: the range is −2^(N−1) to 2^(N−1) − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 5 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into no other integer type; every other conversion is explicit, a `LINT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_LINT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **LINT** | *"LINT 8-byte integer"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), whose printed range there, *"0 to 32,535,129,599,999,999"*, is the date and time display range: the design considerations manual (RM094N, Sept 2025, p.24) gives −2^63 to 2^63−1. On the 5x70 controllers a LINT is copy only (*"You cannot add, subtract, multiply, or divide"*, Logix Designer help v38.01, *LINT data types*); on the 5x80 it is read as an LDT where it meets a time (reference p.878). |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **LINT**, S7-1500 and S7-1200 G2 only; none on the classic S7-1200 or in classic STEP 7 | 64 bits, signed (TIA V21, *Overview of the valid data types*, its family qualifiers). |
| CODESYS | **LINT** | -2^63 to 2^63−1 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | `unverified`: SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR was not re-read for this stanza; the `dint` stanza found only "Word [Signed]" (INT) and "Double Word [Signed]" (DINT) there. |

**Chosen:** `lint`
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the type LINT, and every dialect surveyed that has it spells it so (decision 144); logex lowercases it, as it does `bool` and `dint`. It is the signed integer of 64 bits, its range IEC's footnote c, two's complement for a signed type as Siemens says in words and the conventional reference shows (p.871, 16#FFFF read as −1). Signed and unsigned stay separate types, as in every system read, and IEC's Figure 11 decides what converts without a word (decision 147): never signed into unsigned, nor unsigned into a signed type of its own width, and never narrowing. Its range is the full 64 bits, not the conventional reference's date range. Each of the seven words is reserved in any case where a type word is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8).
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 10 (pp.31–32), Figure 11 (p.67) and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 Tables 10 and 13, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference (Sept 2025) pp.870–875 and design considerations manual (Sept 2025) p.24, and the Logix Designer 33.01 release notes, in the copies that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types* and Siemens A5E02486680-05; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer data types*. ### `usint` — the 8-bit unsigned integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var boxes usint`; `var_global lane usint 3` | 0 to 255, unsigned, 8 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. Declared wherever a dint is: a tag of any section, `var_external` among them, a block's member, a global, located or not, a host's input, a device's value and a force, each checked against its range. `move` widens it into `uint udint ulint int dint lint` (Ed 3 Figure 11); an arithmetic or comparison slot reads a dint alone, so `usint_to_dint` comes first until those instructions take every width (`PLAN.md` M5-4); anything else takes a conversion, `usint_to_sint` among them, which keeps the low bits of a value that does not fit and passes no power on that scan. A literal moved into one, given to a `cal` formal or connected to a var_input of its type must fit it. A `var_external` binds only to a global of exactly its type. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **USINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 6, "Unsigned short integer", N = 8, footnote d: the range is 0 to 2^N − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 6 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into `uint udint ulint int dint lint` and, among the reals, `real` and `lreal`; every other conversion is explicit, a `USINT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_USINT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **USINT** | *"USINT 1-byte unsigned integer 0 to 255"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), for the 5x80 controllers only, since version 32 (33.01 release notes: the types *"were introduced in version 32"*). |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **USINT** in TIA's reference, **USInt** in the S7-1200 manual; none in classic STEP 7 | 0 to 255 (S7-1200 A5E02486680-05 Table 4-17 p.88, *"Integer data types (U = unsigned, S = short, D= double)"*). |
| CODESYS | **USINT** | 0 to 255 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | `unverified`: SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR was not re-read for this stanza; the `dint` stanza found only "Word [Signed]" (INT) and "Double Word [Signed]" (DINT) there. |

**Chosen:** `usint`
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the type USINT, and every dialect surveyed that has it spells it so (decision 144); logex lowercases it, as it does `bool` and `dint`. It is the unsigned integer of 8 bits, its range IEC's footnote d, two's complement for a signed type as Siemens says in words and the conventional reference shows (p.871, 16#FFFF read as −1). Signed and unsigned stay separate types, as in every system read, and IEC's Figure 11 decides what converts without a word (decision 147): never signed into unsigned, nor unsigned into a signed type of its own width, and never narrowing. The unsigned types come too (decision 145), all four, since in logex a width is a range row: a box count can never be negative. Each of the seven words is reserved in any case where a type word is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8).
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 10 (pp.31–32), Figure 11 (p.67) and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 Tables 10 and 13, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference (Sept 2025) pp.870–875 and design considerations manual (Sept 2025) p.24, and the Logix Designer 33.01 release notes, in the copies that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types* and Siemens A5E02486680-05; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer data types*. Mitsubishi was not re-read.
*(Amended 2026-10-22, M5-4, `docs/organisation.md` decision 147: the arithmetic and the comparisons take it as they take a dint, so the conversion to a dint before one is no longer needed: `add` and its kin compute in their destination's type, a source of this type taken where Figure 11 widens it into that type, and a comparison reads it beside an integer of any width by value.)*

### `uint` — the 16-bit unsigned integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var_global fan_hz uint at drive.i.0`; `var_input hz uint` | 0 to 65535, unsigned, 16 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. Declared wherever a dint is: a tag of any section, `var_external` among them, a block's member, a global, located or not, a host's input, a device's value and a force, each checked against its range. `move` widens it into `udint ulint dint lint` (Ed 3 Figure 11); an arithmetic or comparison slot reads a dint alone, so `uint_to_dint` comes first until those instructions take every width (`PLAN.md` M5-4); anything else takes a conversion, `uint_to_int` among them, which keeps the low bits of a value that does not fit and passes no power on that scan. A literal moved into one, given to a `cal` formal or connected to a var_input of its type must fit it. A `var_external` binds only to a global of exactly its type. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **UINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 7, "Unsigned integer", N = 16, footnote d: the range is 0 to 2^N − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 7 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into `udint ulint dint lint` and, among the reals, `real` and `lreal`; every other conversion is explicit, a `UINT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_UINT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **UINT** | *"UINT 2-byte unsigned integer 0 to 65,535"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), for the 5x80 controllers only, since version 32. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **UINT** in TIA's reference, **UInt** in the S7-1200 manual; none in classic STEP 7 | 0 to 65,535 (S7-1200 A5E02486680-05 Table 4-17 p.88). |
| CODESYS | **UINT** | 0 to 65535 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | `unverified`: SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR was not re-read for this stanza; the `dint` stanza found only "Word [Signed]" (INT) and "Double Word [Signed]" (DINT) there. |

**Chosen:** `uint`
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the type UINT, and every dialect surveyed that has it spells it so (decision 144); logex lowercases it, as it does `bool` and `dint`. It is the unsigned integer of 16 bits, its range IEC's footnote d, two's complement for a signed type as Siemens says in words and the conventional reference shows (p.871, 16#FFFF read as −1). Signed and unsigned stay separate types, as in every system read, and IEC's Figure 11 decides what converts without a word (decision 147): never signed into unsigned, nor unsigned into a signed type of its own width, and never narrowing. A 16-bit drive register, 0 to 65,535, is the case decision 145 names: `uint` at a point, moved into a `dint`, is M5-3's done sentence. Each of the seven words is reserved in any case where a type word is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.8).
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 10 (pp.31–32), Figure 11 (p.67) and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 Tables 10 and 13, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference (Sept 2025) pp.870–875 and design considerations manual (Sept 2025) p.24, and the Logix Designer 33.01 release notes, in the copies that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types* and Siemens A5E02486680-05; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer data types*. Mitsubishi was not re-read.
*(Amended 2026-10-22, M5-4, `docs/organisation.md` decision 147: the arithmetic and the comparisons take it as they take a dint, so the conversion to a dint before one is no longer needed: `add` and its kin compute in their destination's type, a source of this type taken where Figure 11 widens it into that type, and a comparison reads it beside an integer of any width by value.)*

### `ulint` — the 64-bit unsigned integer type

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var parts_made ulint` | 0 to 18446744073709551615, unsigned, 64 bits, starting at 0 unless given a value. | IEC 61131-3 | **ULINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.4.2.1 Table 10 feature 9, "Unsigned long integer", N = 64, footnote d: the range is 0 to 2^N − 1, default initial value 0; Ed 2:2003 §2.3.1 Table 10 feature 9 and Table 13. Ed 3 Figure 11 (p.67) lets it convert implicitly into no other integer type; every other conversion is explicit, a `ULINT_TO_<type>` or `<type>_TO_ULINT` function (Table 22 feature 1a, Table 23). |
| Conventional | **ULINT** | *"ULINT 8-byte unsigned integer 0 to 18,446,744,073,709,551,615"* (general instruction reference, Sept 2025, p.873), for the 5x80 controllers only, since version 32. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **ULINT**, S7-1500 and S7-1200 G2 only; none on the classic S7-1200 | *"has a length of 64 bits and contains unsigned numerical values"*, 0 to 18_446_744_073_709_551_615 (TIA V21, *ULINT (64-bit integers)*). |
| CODESYS | **ULINT** | 0 to 2^64−1 (*Integer data types*); default 0. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | | `unverified`: SH(NA)-081264ENG-AR was not re-read for this stanza; the `dint` stanza found only "Word [Signed]" (INT) and "Double Word [Signed]" (DINT) there. |

### `dint_to_int` — convert an integer into another integer type: the family of the 56 integer conversions

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `dint_to_int day_total small_count`; `uint_to_dint fan_hz speed`; `xic run dint_to_sint level trim ote trim_ok` | `<from>_to_<to> src dst`, for every ordered pair of the eight integer types, 56 mnemonics. `src` is a tag, a member or a literal of the `from` type, a literal fitting it, and a tag of another integer type is told the conversion that does the whole job, `sint_to_int` for an `sint` given to `dint_to_int`; `dst` a tag or a member of exactly the `to` type that logic may write. Energised, `dst` takes the source's value and the instruction passes power where the value fits the `to` type; where it does not, `dst` keeps its low bits, N of them for an N-bit target, two's complement for a signed one, and on that scan passes no power, with no fault, as an arithmetic result that wraps (decision 146): `dint_to_sint` of 129 gives −127, `dint_to_usint` of −1 gives 255, `uint_to_int` of 40000 gives −25536. De-energised, it writes nothing and passes no power, as `move`. A widening one, `int_to_dint`, always fits and does what `move` does. What it passes on is settled, IEC's ENO, so anything may follow it on its path. M5-3. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **`<FROM>_TO_<TO>`**, `INT_TO_DINT` and the rest | Typed conversion functions, Ed 3 §6.6.2.5.2 Table 22 feature 1a (*"\*_TO_\*\*"*), p.78, the 56 integer rows of Table 23 (pp.80–81) classed *"Value preserving conversion"* where the target holds every source value and *"Value range errors give an Implementer specific result"* where it does not; Ed 2 §2.5.1.5.1 Table 22 feature 1. *"If the value of the source variable does not fit into the target data type, i.e. the value range is too small, then value of the target variable is Implementer specific."* (Ed 3 §6.6.2.5.3, p.79). Ed 3 also has an overloaded `TO_<TO>` (feature 1b). A function's ENO is IEC's EN/ENO rule (Ed 3 §6.6.1.5). |
| Conventional | none: **MOV** converts to its destination's type | No conversion instruction between integer types; an instruction converts each operand. *"To convert a DINT value to a SINT or INT value, the controller truncates the upper portion of the DINT and stores the lower bits that fit in the data type. If the value is too large the conversion generates an overflow."*, DINT 16#0001_0081 (65,665) to INT 16#0081 (129) to SINT 16#81 (−127) (general instructions reference, Sept 2025, p.872); *"Signed integer to unsigned integer: If the source value is too big to fit into destination, the controller stores what it can and may produce an overflow."*, and the reverse the same (p.874): what it stores for a negative value into an unsigned type is `unverified`. Rung-condition-out is rung-condition-in; the overflow sets S:V. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | **CONV** box; SCL `DINT_TO_SINT(...)` | S7-1500 and S7-1200 G2: *"a conversion takes place even in the event of an overflow, but the values are truncated and the ENO enable output is set to "0""*, and *"If a negative value is converted to an unsigned target data type, the enable output ENO is set to "0""* (TIA V21, *Overview of data type conversion (S7-1500, S7-1200 G2)* and *Explicit conversion of DINT*). The classic S7-1200's CONV: *"Result exceeds valid range for OUT data type — OUT is set to the IN value"*, ENO 0 (S7-1200 manual collection V20, *CONV*). With the per-block IEC check, on by default, no signed type converts implicitly into an unsigned one. |
| CODESYS | **`DINT_TO_SINT`** and the rest, `<TYPE>_TO_<TYPE>` | *"If the number to be converted exceeds the range limit, then the first bytes of the number are ignored."* (*Integer conversion*); its operators page calls an operand outside the target's range *"undefined"*. Implicit narrowing is allowed, with warnings C0195 and C0196 for a sign change. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | one instruction per conversion, **DBL** (16 to 32 bits) and **WORD** (32 to 16) | Carried from the Data movement and conversion table above, not re-read for this stanza. |

**Chosen:** `<from>_to_<to>`, lowercased, for each of the 56 ordered pairs.
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names each conversion, typed, `INT_TO_DINT` (Table 22 feature 1a), and CODESYS and Siemens' SCL spell them the same, so logex takes IEC's typed names lowercased (decision 148), not Ed 3's overloaded `TO_<type>`, which the design recommended and the maintainer did not take: the name says both types, as `docs/naming.md`'s Data movement table planned with `int_to_real`. Out of range the value wraps, its low bits kept, as the conventional controller truncates (p.872, the −127 this item pins) and CODESYS drops the high bytes, and passes no power, as the S7-1500 sets ENO 0: the S7-1200's reading of an arithmetic overflow, which decision 146 took for every integer result, so a conversion and an `add` that leave the type do one thing. IEC leaves the value to the implementer. A conversion is the one way a value narrows or changes sign, since `move` and every slot take only what Figure 11 widens (decision 147), and each refusal names the conversion to use, which is why the 56 land with the widths (`PLAN.md` M5-3). One stanza for the family, each member named, rather than 56 that say the same thing (§4.12's routine choice); `naming_test.exs` reads the members below as data. Each is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, as a mnemonic is.
**Members:** `sint_to_int`, `sint_to_dint`, `sint_to_lint`, `sint_to_usint`, `sint_to_uint`, `sint_to_udint`, `sint_to_ulint`, `int_to_sint`, `int_to_dint`, `int_to_lint`, `int_to_usint`, `int_to_uint`, `int_to_udint`, `int_to_ulint`, `dint_to_sint`, `dint_to_int`, `dint_to_lint`, `dint_to_usint`, `dint_to_uint`, `dint_to_udint`, `dint_to_ulint`, `lint_to_sint`, `lint_to_int`, `lint_to_dint`, `lint_to_usint`, `lint_to_uint`, `lint_to_udint`, `lint_to_ulint`, `usint_to_sint`, `usint_to_int`, `usint_to_dint`, `usint_to_lint`, `usint_to_uint`, `usint_to_udint`, `usint_to_ulint`, `uint_to_sint`, `uint_to_int`, `uint_to_dint`, `uint_to_lint`, `uint_to_usint`, `uint_to_udint`, `uint_to_ulint`, `udint_to_sint`, `udint_to_int`, `udint_to_dint`, `udint_to_lint`, `udint_to_usint`, `udint_to_uint`, `udint_to_ulint`, `ulint_to_sint`, `ulint_to_int`, `ulint_to_dint`, `ulint_to_lint`, `ulint_to_usint`, `ulint_to_uint`, `ulint_to_udint`.
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 §6.6.1.5 (p.63), §6.6.2.5.2–6.6.2.5.3 and Tables 22–23 (pp.78–81), and IEC 61131-3:2003 §2.5.1.5.1 and Table 22, read directly in the copies `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21); the conventional family's general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.870–875, in the copy that pass saved; TIA V21 (11/2025) *Data types*, and the S7-1200 manual collection V20, *CONV*; CODESYS Development System help, *Integer conversion* and the operators page, as that pass read them. Mitsubishi was not re-read.

*(Amended 2026-10-22, the maintainer's answers to M5-3's questions, `docs/organisation.md` decisions 176 and 178: `src` may be of the `from` type or of any type Ed 3 Figure 11 widens into it, as an input of IEC's typed functions takes one (Table 21 Example 2, `ADD_REAL` with an INT), so `dint_to_int` takes an `sint`, an `int` or a `usint` and writes what the dint's value would; a source of a type that does not widen into `from` is told the conversion from its own type, `udint_to_int` for a `udint` given to `dint_to_int`, or `move` where its type is the `to` type; `dst` stays exactly the `to` type. A conversion of a literal, `dint_to_sint 129 trim`, compiles with the warning an all-literal `add` draws, "its result never changes". MatIEC's exact typing is no longer logex's.)*

### `ctu_int` — up counter of 16-bit signed integers: a type word

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var boxes ctu_int`; `xic eye ctu boxes 12` | A type word only, declaring an up counter whose `pv` and `cv` are ints, -32768 to 32767, a `var` with no initial value: `ctu`'s members and every rule of `ctu` (its own stanza) but for its type, and the instruction `ctu` runs it. Its preset, the literal on the instruction, is judged in this type, 0 to 32767, so a larger one is refused at its line; on a rising edge `cv` gains 1, or at 32767 gains nothing and `ov` is set, which stays until `r`, decision 71 by width. A `move` into `boxes.pv` takes a source that widens into an int (Ed 3 Figure 11). No instruction is named after it: the one that runs `ctu` runs it, its unit a count as `ctu`'s. An edit that changes a counter's type, `ctu` to `ctu_int` among them, is refused, as any type change is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.9). M5-5. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **CTU_INT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.4 Table 45, the typed CTU of `PV` and `CV` of type INT, feature 1a, IEC's plain CTU, which logex's `ctu` is not: logex's is CTU_DINT; `PVmax` and `PVmin` *"Implementer specific"* (the Table 45 NOTE). Ed 2:2003 Table 36 has the same typed family (`docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2). |
| Conventional | none: the **COUNTER** structure is DINT alone | `.PRE` and `.ACC` are DINT (general instructions ref., pp.93–94; `docs/organisation.md` decision 158's reading of RM018A p.111). There is no counter of another width. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **CTU** with a data type chosen for `PV` and `CV` | Whether TIA's CTU takes INT: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **CTU**, `PV` and `CV` of type WORD | Whether its standard library has an INT variant: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | FB library CTU / CTU_E | Its typed variants: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |

**Chosen:** `ctu_int`, a type word.
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the typed counter CTU_INT, lowercased as every IEC word logex takes (decision 158, as the maintainer took typed variants on 2026-10-21). The instructions stay `ctu` and `ctd`, each running every counter of its kind, so a rung says no width and its preset is judged in its instance's type (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice); twelve more mnemonics would add no rule of their own. The count stops at the type's limits, decision 71 by width, as the maintainer answered §4.12's fifth question, where Logix's CTUD rolls over; an unsigned down counter so stops at 0. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ctu_int` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 45 (pp.113–114) and IEC 61131-3:2003 Table 36, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21) and `docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2 records them; the conventional family's general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.92–96, as the `ctu` stanza read it. The Siemens, CODESYS and Mitsubishi rows were not re-read.

|---|---|---|

A `move` into `boxes.pv` takes a source that widens into an lint (Ed 3 Figure 11). No instruction is named after it: the one that runs `ctu` runs it, its unit a count as `ctu`'s. **Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the typed counter CTU_LINT, lowercased as every IEC word logex takes (decision 158, as the maintainer took typed variants on 2026-10-21). The instructions stay `ctu` and `ctd`, each running every counter of its kind, so a rung says no width and its preset is judged in its instance's type (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice); twelve more mnemonics would add no rule of their own. The count stops at the type's limits, decision 71 by width, as the maintainer answered §4.12's fifth question, where Logix's CTUD rolls over; an unsigned down counter so stops at 0. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ctu_lint` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 45 (pp.113–114) and IEC 61131-3:2003 Table 36, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21) and `docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2 records them; the conventional family's general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.92–96, as the `ctu` stanza read it. The Siemens, CODESYS and Mitsubishi rows were not re-read.

### `ctu_udint` — up counter of 32-bit unsigned integers: a type word

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var boxes ctu_udint`; `xic eye ctu boxes 12` | A type word only, declaring an up counter whose `pv` and `cv` are udints, 0 to 4294967295, a `var` with no initial value: `ctu`'s members and every rule of `ctu` (its own stanza) but for its type, and the instruction `ctu` runs it. Its preset, the literal on the instruction, is judged in this type, 0 to 4294967295, so a larger one is refused at its line; on a rising edge `cv` gains 1, or at 4294967295 gains nothing and `ov` is set, which stays until `r`, decision 71 by width. A `move` into `boxes.pv` takes a source that widens into a udint (Ed 3 Figure 11). No instruction is named after it: the one that runs `ctu` runs it, its unit a count as `ctu`'s. An edit that changes a counter's type, `ctu` to `ctu_udint` among them, is refused, as any type change is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.9). M5-5. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **CTU_UDINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.4 Table 45, the typed CTU of `PV` and `CV` of type UDINT, feature 1d, *"Same as 1a"* but for the type; `PVmax` and `PVmin` *"Implementer specific"* (the Table 45 NOTE). Ed 2:2003 Table 36 has the same typed family (`docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2). |
| Conventional | none: the **COUNTER** structure is DINT alone | `.PRE` and `.ACC` are DINT (general instructions ref., pp.93–94; `docs/organisation.md` decision 158's reading of RM018A p.111). There is no counter of another width. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **CTU** with a data type chosen for `PV` and `CV` | Whether TIA's CTU takes UDINT: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **CTU**, `PV` and `CV` of type WORD | Whether its standard library has a UDINT variant: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | FB library CTU / CTU_E | Its typed variants: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |

**Chosen:** `ctu_udint`, a type word.
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the typed counter CTU_UDINT, lowercased as every IEC word logex takes (decision 158, as the maintainer took typed variants on 2026-10-21). The instructions stay `ctu` and `ctd`, each running every counter of its kind, so a rung says no width and its preset is judged in its instance's type (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice); twelve more mnemonics would add no rule of their own. The count stops at the type's limits, decision 71 by width, as the maintainer answered §4.12's fifth question, where Logix's CTUD rolls over; an unsigned down counter so stops at 0. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ctu_udint` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 45 (pp.113–114) and IEC 61131-3:2003 Table 36, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21) and `docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2 records them; the conventional family's general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.92–96, as the `ctu` stanza read it. The Siemens, CODESYS and Mitsubishi rows were not re-read.

### `ctd_int` — down counter of 16-bit signed integers: a type word

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var hopper ctd_int`; `xic portion ctd hopper 40` | A type word only, declaring a down counter whose `pv` and `cv` are ints, -32768 to 32767, a `var` with no initial value: `ctd`'s members and every rule of `ctd` (its own stanza) but for its type, and the instruction `ctd` runs it. Its preset, the literal on the instruction, is judged in this type, 0 to 32767, so a larger one is refused at its line; on a rising edge `cv` loses 1, or at -32768 loses nothing and `un` is set, which stays until `ld`, decision 71 by width. A `move` into `hopper.pv` takes a source that widens into an int (Ed 3 Figure 11). No instruction is named after it: the one that runs `ctd` runs it, its unit a count as `ctd`'s. An edit that changes a counter's type, `ctd` to `ctd_int` among them, is refused, as any type change is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.9). M5-5. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **CTD_INT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.4 Table 45, the typed CTD of `PV` and `CV` of type INT, feature 2a (the letters c to e of CTD and CTUD read by the pattern of CTU's, f1c `CTU_LINT`, f1d `CTU_UDINT` and f1e `CTU_ULINT`: `unverified` for this feature letter), IEC's plain CTD, which logex's `ctd` is not: logex's is CTD_DINT; `PVmax` and `PVmin` *"Implementer specific"* (the Table 45 NOTE). Ed 2:2003 Table 36 has the same typed family (`docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2). |
| Conventional | none: the **COUNTER** structure is DINT alone | `.PRE` and `.ACC` are DINT (general instructions ref., pp.93–94; `docs/organisation.md` decision 158's reading of RM018A p.111). There is no counter of another width. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **CTD** with a data type chosen for `PV` and `CV` | Whether TIA's CTD takes INT: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **CTD**, `PV` and `CV` of type WORD | Whether its standard library has an INT variant: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | FB library CTD / CTD_E | Its typed variants: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |

The instructions stay `ctu` and `ctd`, each running every counter of its kind, so a rung says no width and its preset is judged in its instance's type (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice); twelve more mnemonics would add no rule of their own. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ctd_int` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").

| IEC 61131-3 | **CTUD_LINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.4 Table 45, the typed CTUD of `PV` and `CV` of type LINT, feature 3c (the letters c to e of CTD and CTUD read by the pattern of CTU's, f1c `CTU_LINT`, f1d `CTU_UDINT` and f1e `CTU_ULINT`: `unverified` for this feature letter), as feature 3a but for the type; `PVmax` and `PVmin` *"Implementer specific"* (the Table 45 NOTE). Ed 2:2003 Table 36 has the same typed family (`docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2). |
| Conventional | none: the **COUNTER** structure is DINT alone | `.PRE` and `.ACC` are DINT (general instructions ref., pp.93–94; `docs/organisation.md` decision 158's reading of RM018A p.111). There is no counter of another width. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **CTUD** with a data type chosen for `PV` and `CV` | Whether TIA's CTUD takes LINT: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **CTUD**, `PV` and `CV` of type WORD | Whether its standard library has an LINT variant: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | FB library CTUD / CTUD_E | Its typed variants: `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |

**Chosen:** `ctud_lint`, a type word.
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the typed counter CTUD_LINT, lowercased as every IEC word logex takes (decision 158, as the maintainer took typed variants on 2026-10-21). The instructions stay `ctu` and `ctd`, each running every counter of its kind, so a rung says no width and its preset is judged in its instance's type (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice); twelve more mnemonics would add no rule of their own. The count stops at the type's limits, decision 71 by width, as the maintainer answered §4.12's fifth question, where Logix's CTUD rolls over; an unsigned down counter so stops at 0. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ctud_lint` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 45 (pp.113–114) and IEC 61131-3:2003 Table 36, as Milestone 5's design pass read them (2026-10-21) and `docs/instruction-sets.md` §3.2 records them; the conventional family's general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.92–96, as the `ctu` stanza read it. The Siemens, CODESYS and Mitsubishi rows were not re-read.

### `ctu_dint` — the up counter of dints: another word for `ctu`

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var boxes ctu_dint`, the same as `var boxes ctu`; `xic eye ctu boxes 12` | A type word only, an alias of `ctu`: a declaration with either word gives the one type, `Logex.FbType.ctu/0`, and keeps no spelling, so `var boxes ctu_dint` and `var boxes ctu` compile to equal programs, and every rule of `ctu`'s stanza is its own. Every message and warning names the instance by the plain word, `a ctu`, whichever word declared it: its article, the compiler's refusals of a counter, the warning for a counter no instruction runs, the event task's counter warning and an edit's refusal of a type change; and an edit from `ctu` to `ctu_dint` changes no type, so it is neither refused nor reported (decision 163). M5-5. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **CTU_DINT** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.4 Table 45 feature 1b, *"Same as 1a"* with `PV` and `CV` of type DINT; IEC's plain CTU is CTU_INT (feature 1a), where logex's `ctu` has been CTU_DINT since M3-1. |
| Conventional | the **COUNTER** structure, DINT | `.PRE` and `.ACC` are DINT (general instructions ref., pp.93–94): the one counter there is. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **CTU** with DINT chosen for `PV` and `CV` | `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **CTU**, WORD | Whether its library has a DINT variant: `unverified`. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | FB library CTU / CTU_E | `unverified`. |

**Chosen:** `ctu_dint`, a type word, the alias of `ctu`.
**Why:** Decision 163, as the maintainer answered §4.12's sixth question on 2026-10-21: a programmer used to IEC's typed counters writes `ctu_dint`, IEC's name for the very type logex's `ctu` declares, so the word is taken as a second word for it, not a type of its own. Its cost is a message that names a word the declaration does not hold, `a ctu` for `var boxes ctu_dint`, which keeps two declarations of one thing equal as data, so neither `Logex.FbType.same?/2` nor an edit's type comparison has a spelling to excuse. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 Table 45 (pp.113–114), as Milestone 5's design pass read it (2026-10-21); the conventional general instructions reference, Sept 2025, pp.92–96, as the `ctu` stanza read it.

### `ton_ltime` — on-delay timer counted in nanoseconds: a type word

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| logex | `var cure ton_ltime`; `xic press ton cure 2592000000000000`; `xic cure.dn ote cured` | A type word only, declaring an on-delay timer whose `.pre` and `.acc` are `lint` counts of **nanoseconds**, a `var` with no initial value: `ton`'s members and every rule of `ton` (its own stanza) but for its unit, and the instruction `ton` runs it. Its preset, the literal on the instruction, is in nanoseconds, 0 to 9223372036854775807, about 292 years, `2592000000000000` above being 30 days. **The unit is the instance's, not the rung's:** `ton t 2000000` is 2,000,000 ms, about 33 minutes, where `t` is a `ton`, and 2 ms where it is a `ton_ltime`. **The resolution is a millisecond:** logex's clock and every scan's elapsed time are whole milliseconds, so `.acc` gains 1,000,000 ns for each millisecond since this `ton` last ran, and a preset that is no whole number of milliseconds, `1500000`, is done at the first scan whose `.acc` reaches it, at the next whole millisecond, 2 ms, and is warned of. A `move` into `cure.pre` takes a source that widens into a lint (Ed 3 Figure 11). An edit from `ton` to `ton_ltime`, or back, is refused, as any type change is (`docs/organisation.md` §4.9). M5-6. |
| IEC 61131-3 | **TON_LTIME** | Ed 3:2013 §6.6.3.5.5 Table 46 feature 2c, *"On-delay using LTIME"*, p.115, beside the overloaded TON (2a) and TON_TIME (2b): *"The standard timer function blocks may be used overloaded with TIME or LTIME, or the base data type for the standard timer may be specified as TIME or LTIME"* (p.114). LTIME is *"a signed 64-bit integer with unit of nanoseconds"* (Table 10 footnote m, pp.32–33). Ed 2:2003 has no LTIME. |
| Conventional | the **TIMER** structure, DINT milliseconds | `.PRE` and `.ACC` DINT, *"(1 millisecond units)"*, as the `ton` stanza reads it; LTIME, 64-bit nanoseconds, is a type on the 5x80 controllers (`docs/organisation.md` decision 158's reading of RM094N p.13). Whether a timer there takes an LTIME preset, as the Timers table's TIMER_T suggests: `unverified`. |
| Siemens STEP 7 / TIA Portal LAD | TIA **TON** with LTIME chosen for `PT` and `ET`, an IEC_LTIMER, on the S7-1500 | `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| CODESYS | **LTON**, of its 64-bit standard library | `unverified`, not re-read for this stanza. |
| Mitsubishi GX Works | none found | `unverified`. |

**Chosen:** `ton_ltime`, a type word.
**Why:** Rule 1: IEC names the timer TON_LTIME, lowercased as every IEC word logex takes (decision 158, as the maintainer took typed variants on 2026-10-21). The instruction stays `ton`, running every on-delay timer, so a rung says no unit, its preset judged in its instance's type and unit (`docs/organisation.md` §4.12's routine choice): that costs a rung whose meaning a declaration changes a million-fold, which the stanza says plainly, and which the warning for a preset that is no whole number of milliseconds catches only in part. The TIME and LTIME types themselves stay deferred, so the preset is a plain integer, ns here as ms for `ton`. A finer clock would put nanoseconds into the core's `now`, a later item; until then a long timer is a `ton` with 63 bits of range and a millisecond's resolution, as IEC leaves the timer's resolution to the implementer. It is reserved in any case in every `.ld` file, so a tag, label or instance named `ton_ltime` stops compiling (§4.12, "Reserved words").
**Checked:** 2026-10-22. IEC 61131-3:2013 §6.6.3.5.5 and Table 46 (pp.114–115), and Table 10 and its footnote m (pp.32–33), read directly in the copy `docs/instruction-sets.md` §9 lists; the conventional rows as the `ton` stanza and decision 158 read them. The Siemens, CODESYS and Mitsubishi rows were not re-read.

*(Amended 2026-10-26, M5-16, checked against the extract of 1756-RM094N-EN-P, Sept 2025: the Conventional row's LTIME on the 5x80 controllers is on p.21, the 5380 and 5580 chapter's date and time data types, and p.25 for the 5480; p.13, which the row and decision 158 cite, is the 5590 chapter's. 1756-RM018A p.111 adds a TIMER_T structure whose `.ACC` is a TIME with microsecond resolution beside the DINT-millisecond TIMER, so the row's "Whether a timer there takes an LTIME preset" stays `unverified`.)*

### `tof_ltime` — off-delay timer counted in nanoseconds: a type word

| Dialect | Name there | Notes |

An edit from `tof` to `tof_ltime` is refused, as any type change is. M5-6. |

| Mitsubishi GX Works | none found | `unverified`. |
